```text
(a) The functions conferred upon the President by sections 122(e), 298(a), 451, 
504(b), 613(a), 614(a), 620(a), 620(d), 620(x), 620A, 620C(c), 621(a), 622(b), 622(c), 
633(a), 633(b), 640B, 662(a), and 663(b) of the A ct.
(b) The functions conferred upon the President by sections 402, 405(a), 406 and 
407 of the IDC A ct of 1979.
(c) H ie  functions conferred upon the President by the A ct and section 408(b) of 
the Mutual Security A ct of 1954 with respect to the appointment of officers 
required to be appointed by and with the advice and consent of the Senate 
and with respect to the appointment of officers pursuant to sections 233(b) and 
624(c) of the Act.
(d) The functions conferred upon the President with respect to determinations, 
certifications, directives, or transfers of funds, as the case may be, by sections 
303, 481(a), 505(d)(2)(A), 505(d)(3), 506(a), 515(f), 604(a), 610, 614(c), 632(b), 
633A, 659, 663(a), 669(b)(1) and 670(b)(1) of the A ct.
(e) The following-described functions conferred upon the President:
(1) Those under section 503(a) that relate to findings: P rovid ed , that the 
Secretary of State, in the implementation of the functions delegated to him 
under section 505(a)(1), (a)(4), and (e) of the A ct, is authorized to find, in the 
case of a proposed transfer of a defense article or related training or a related 
defense service by a foreign country or international organization to a foreign 
country or international organization not otherwise eligible under section 
503(a) o f the A ct, whether the proposed transfer will strengthen the security of 
the United States and promote world peace.
(2) Those under section 505(b) in respect of countries that do not agree to the 
conditions set forth therein.
(3) That under section 614(b) with respect to determining any provisions of 
law to be disregarded to achieve the purpose of that section.
(4) That under the second sentence of section 654(c) with respect to the 
publication in the Federal Register of any findings or determination reserved 
to the President: P ro vid ed , that any officer to whom there is delegated the 
function of making any finding or determination within the purview of section 
654(a) is also authorized to reach the conclusion specified in performance of 
the function delegated to him.
(f) Those with respect to determinations under sections 103(b) (first proviso), 
104, and 203 of the Mutual Defense Assistance Control A ct of 1951 (22 U .S.C . 
1611b(b}, 1611c, and 1612b).
(g) That under section 523(d) o f the Mutual Security A ct of 1954 (22 U .S.C . 
1783(d)).
(h) Those under section 607 o f the Foreign Assistance and Related Programs 
Appropriations A ct, 1979 (92 S ta t 1591,1601), with respect to findings.
1-702. Su b seq u en t A m endm ents. Functions conferred upon the President by 
subsequent amendments to the A c t are delegated to the Director only insofar 
as they do not relate directly and necessarily to the conduct of programs and 
activities that either the President or an agency other than ID C A  is authorized 
to administer pursuant to express reservation or delegation of authorities in a 
statute or in this or another Executive order.
1-8. FU N D S
1-801. A llo ca tio n  o f Fu n ds. Funds appropriated or otherwise made available 
to the President for carrying out the A ct shall be deemed to be allocated 
without any further action of the President, as follows:
(a) There are allocated to the Director (1) all funds made available for carrying 
out the A ct except those made available for carrying out Part II of the Act 
(other than chapter 4 thereof), section 481 of the A ct, and section 637(b) of the 
A ct, and (2) all funds made available for carrying out title IV  of the IDC A ct of 
1979.

=== PDF page 25 of 256 ===
Federal Register / Vol. 44, No. 192 / Tuesday, October 2, 1979 / Presidential Documents 56679
(b) There are allocated to the Secretary of Defense funds made available for 
carrying out Part II of the A ct (except chapters 4 and 6 thereof).
(c) There are allocated to the Secretary of State funds made available for 
carrying out sections 481 and 637(b) and chapter 6 of Part II of the A ct.
1-802. R ea llo ca tion  o f  Funds. The Director of ID C A , the Secretary of Defense, 
and the Secretary of State may allocate or transfer as appropriate any funds 
received under subsections (a), (b), and (c), respectively of section 1-801 of 
this order, to any agency or part thereof for obligation or expenditure thereby 
consistent with applicable law.
1-9. GEN ERAL P R O V ISIO N S
1-901. Definition. A s used in this order, the word “ function” includes any duty, 
obligation, power, authority, responsibility, right, privilege, discretion, or activ­
ity*
1-902. R eferen ces to O rd ers and A c ts . Except as may for any reason be 
inappropriate:
(a) References in this order or in any other Executive order to (1) the Foreign 
Assistance A ct of 1961 (including references herein to “ the A ct” ), (2) unre­
pealed provisions of the Mutual Security A ct of 1954, or (3) any other act that 
relates to the subject of this order shall be deemed to include references to 
any subsequent amendments thereto.
(b) References in any prior Executive order to the Mutual Security A ct of 1954 
or any provisions thereof shall be deemed to be references to the A ct or the 
corresponding provision, if any, thereof.
(c) References in this order to provisions of any appropriation A ct, and 
references in any other Executive order to provisions of any appropriation A ct 
related to the subject of this order shall be deemed to include references to 
any hereafter-enacted provisions of law  that are the same or substantially the 
same as such appropriation A ct provisions, respectively.
(d) References in this order or in any other Executive order to this order or to 
any provision thereof shall be deemed to include references thereto, respec­
tively, as amended from time to time.
(e) References in any prior Executive order not superseded by this order to 
any provisions of any Executive order so superseded shall hereafter be 
deemed to be references to the corresponding provisions, if any, of this order.
1-903. P rior E x ecu tiv e O rd ers, (a) The following are revoked:
(1) Executive Order No. 10973 of November 3,1961, as amended;
(2) section 2(a) of Executive Order No. 11579 of January 19,1971; and
(3) Executive Order No. 10893 of November 8,1960.
(b) The following are amended:
(1) section 3(a) of Executive Order No. 11846 of March 27,1975, as amended, 
by adding the following new paragraph (12) after paragraph (11):
"(12) The Director of the United States International Development Coopera­
tion Agency” ;
(2) section 1-202 of Executive Order 12065 of June 28, 1978, by striking out

out "the Administrator of the Agency for International Development” and 
inserting in lieu thereof “ the Director of the United States International

(4) section 3 of Executive Order 10900 of January 5,1961, by adding thereto the 
following new subsection:

(5) section 4 of Executive Order 11223 of M ay 12,1965, by inserting immediate­

1-904. Sa vin g P ro visio n s. Except to the extent inconsistent with this order, all 
delegations of authority, determinations, authorizations, regulations, rulings, 
certificates, orders, directives, contracts, agreements, and other actions made, 
issued, or entered into with respect to any function affected by this order and 
not revoked, superseded, or otherwise made inapplicable before the date of 
this order, shall continue in full force and effect until amended, modified, or 
terminated by appropriate authority.
1-905. E ffe c tiv e  D ate. The provisions of this order shall become effective as of 
October 1,1979.
THE W H ITE H O U SE ,

By the authority vested in me as President of the United States of America by

ment Association Act, the Asian Development Bank Act, Public Law 95-118,

ation Agency. A s the principal international development advisor to the

shall advise both the Secretary of the Treasury and the appropriate United 
States representatives to the International Bank for Reconstruction and Devel­
opment, the International Development Association, the International Finance 
Corporation, the Inter-American Development Bank, the Asian Development 
Bank, and the African Development Fund on the development aspects of 
matters relating to those institutions and their activities.” .
```

```text
FEDERALREGISTER
VOLUME 24 1 9 3 4  c , ^
Washington, Tuesday, October 13, 1959
NUMBER 200
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 10843
CREATING A BOARD OF INQUIRY  
TO REPORT ON A LABOR DIS­
PUTE AFFECTING THE STEEL IN­
DUSTRY OF THE UNITED STATES
WHEREAS there exists a labor dispute 
between certain corporations or organ­
izations in the United States producing 
or fabricating steel or the components 
thereof and certain of their employees 
represented by the United Steel Workers 
of America, AFL-CIO; and
WHEREAS such dispute has resulted 
in a strike which, in my opinion, affects 
a substantial part of the steel industry, 
an industry engaged in trade, commerce, 
and transportation among the several 
States and with foreign nations, and in 
the production of goods for commerce, 
and which strike will, if permitted to 
continue, imperil the national health 
and safety:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 206 
of the Labor-Management Relations Act, 
1947,61 Stat. 155 (29 U.S.C. 176), I hereby 
create a Board of Inquiry, consisting of 
George W. Taylor of Pennsylvania, 
Chairman, John Perkins of Delaware and 
Paul N. Lehoczky of Ohio as members 
to inquire into the issues involved in 
such dispute.
The Board shall have powers and 
duties as set forth in Title II of such Act. 
The Board shall report to the President 
in accordance with the provisions of sec­
tion 206 of such Act on or before October 16,1959.
Upon submission of its report, tl 
Board shall continue in existence to pe 
form such other functions as may be r 
Quired under such Act, until the Boa: 
ls terminated by the President.
D w ig h t d . E is e n h o w e r 
T he  W h it e H o u se,
October 9,1959.
[PR. Doc. 59-8651; Piled, Oct. 12, 1959; 
8:46 a.m.]

and
WHEREAS, by reason of the transfer

project is now being administered pur­
suant to Title III of the Bankhead-Jones

WHEREAS by Executive Order No.

lands within such project were subject to

to the provisions of Executive Order No.

WHEREAS it appears that such lands

and that it would be in the public in­
terest to include them in and reserve

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the
```

```text
Presidential Documents
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 10989
AMENDMENT OF EXECUTIVE ORDER NO. 101681  OF OCTOBER 11,  
1950, AS AMENDED, PRESCRIBING REGULATIONS RELATING TO THE  
RIGHT OF ENLISTED MEMBERS OF THE UNIFORMED SERVICES TO  
ADDITIONAL PAY FOR SEA AND FOREIGN DUTY
By virtue of the authority vested in me by section 206 of the Career 
Compensation Act of 1949, as amended (37 U.S.C. 237), and as Presi­
dent of the United States and Commander in C h ief.  of the armed 
forces of the United States, it is ordered that Executive Order No. 
10168 of October 11,1950, as amended, be further amended as follows:
1. Section 2 (a ) is amended by adding the following new clauses at 
the end thereof;
“ (5) W hile assigned to an artificial island (such as a Texas Tower) 
located on the outer Continental Shelf outside the territorial waters 
of the United States pursuant to orders issued by competent authority, 
including periods not in excess of fifteen consecutive days each while 
on duty ashore or temporarily based ashore.
“ (6) W hile on an artificial island (such as a Texas Tower) located 
on the outer Continental Shelf outside the territorial waters of the 
United States pursuant. to orders issued by competent authority 
although based or stationed ashore, but only when such duty is eight 
consecutive days or more in duration in each case.”
2. Section 3 is amended by striking out the words “ and (3 )” and 
inserting the words “ , (3 ), (5 ), and (6 )” in place thereof.
The amendments made by this order shajl be effective as of the first 
day of the first month after the month in which this order is issued.
J ohn  F. K ennedy
T he  W hite  H ouse,
January 22, 1962.
[F.R. Doc. 62-883 ; Filed, Jan. 23,1962 ; 12 :39 p.m.]
115 F.R. 6877 ; 3 CFR, 1949-1953 Comp., p. 1071.

```

govinfo PDF text layer of the official page, grade original_representation_unverified), headed Executive Order 10319 and signed January 4, 1952. The order creates a three-member emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate the dispute between Northwest Airlines, Inc. and certain of its employees represented by the International Association of Machinists, requires the board to report within thirty days from the date of the order, and a sentence requiring the board to comply with section 502 of the Defense Production Act of 1950, as amended. Inventory: the order is one operative unit; the creation of the board, its thirty-day report deadline, the non-derogation sentence and the Defense Production Act compliance sentence and the status-quo sentence restating section 10 are all within the single whole-order clause confirmed here. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held government printing) revokes the numbered orders in its table, and any amendments thereto; its No. Subject table lists 10319 with a subject line matching this order (creating an emergency board to investigate a airline labor dispute). The enacting provision has no stated effective date, so the revocation is read as effective on signing, 1986-02-25; the Federal Register publication date 1986-03-03 and the filing date are not effective dates. The machine dossier did not locate the revoking provision for this pair; the held text of EO 12553 does contain the row for this order (number followed by a subject line) in the No. Subject table on its page 10, and the enacting sentence precedes the table, so the whole held EO 12553 text is cited as the ending source. The thirty-day or stated report date and the end of any statutory status-quo period are deadlines and spent facts, not ends (owner ruling 2026-10-21 on report deadlines): the order stayed on the books until express revocation. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches (coverage notes read; no coverage warning) show no instrument other than EO 12553 revoking, superseding or amending this order; the held-text citation search found citations of this number only in printings of the order itself, orders printed on the same page, Federal Register index or codification-guide material and (for some) National Mediation Board report front matter.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) The thirty-day report deadline is a deadline, not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21): the order does not end when the board reports, nor when the statutory thirty-day status-quo period after the report runs out; actual report and standstill dates are spent facts that no reviewed event record in this packet supplies, so they are disclosed here rather than recorded as later events. (2) The statutory standstill in section 10 of the Railway Labor Act is the statute's own effect and not a separate command of this order. (3) No later order amends, supersedes or revokes the order before EO 12553 in the held corpus (OFR catalog and number-citation searches). EO 12553 is the first held instrument that revokes it. No earlier end is shown, so none is claimed. The sentence requiring compliance with section 502 of the Defense Production Act of 1950 is a condition on how the board performs its functions; the statute is not held in this repository, so no expiry of that statute is shown and none is claimed as an earlier end.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order has no command independent of the board's creation and reporting. The thirty-day report requirement and the status-quo sentence are parts of the same board-creating order and share its end; the status-quo sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, whose force comes from that statute. The Defense Production Act compliance sentence is a condition on the board's performance, not a separate command with its own sunset shown in held evidence.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings and residual effects: the held text of EO 12553 contains no savings or exception clause attached to the revocation list (the enacting provision is a single sentence followed by the table, and the only effective-date or exception wording found anywhere in the order is outside it). The board's work, report and any agreement reached are completed past events rather than continuing commands of this order; no held source shows any continuing independent effect.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: EO 12553 revokes the order by number and the held table row matches its subject; the enacting provision reaches any amendments thereto and has no exception, so it reaches the whole order, not part. The order has no amendment in the held corpus.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"6b067cb2b9d1ca895a51a7fa83f715f8f67dde108b6d8ee7dfd009452b533611","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:21","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"6b067cb2b9d1ca895a51a7fa83f715f8f67dde108b6d8ee7dfd009452b533611","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:21","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10319#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"e15ec46b2bebf8a30bc298c7dd0f7c44b437894525faeb2520880a38ffde7bc3","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10319.txt","sha256":"e15ec46b2bebf8a30bc298c7dd0f7c44b437894525faeb2520880a38ffde7bc3","state":"match"},"sha256":"d60d05278b014bfc8bb0c0354efc3b70e35182a65e9f13473568d02fa0389db8","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10319.txt","text_sha256":"e15ec46b2bebf8a30bc298c7dd0f7c44b437894525faeb2520880a38ffde7bc3"}]}]}

check independent commands and surviving effects.","Investigate cessation before the later revocation/ending instrument; locating that instrument does not establish the first end."],"scope_id":"EO:10634#whole","state":"requires_clause_specific_research"}],"existing_lifecycle_questions_notice":"Current dossier research prompts, not new gap findings or overrides of prior claim/review conclusions.","new_legal_gaps_invented":[],"prior_differences_require_scope_source_proposition_review":true},"selection_and_checkpoint_records":[],"state":"prior_work_located","status_holds":[],"status_research":[],"unattached_status_reviews":[],"unfinished_work":[],"versions":[{"claim_sha256":"fe18a48e499135d56aa7d220770af9b0c87eff680e7c41d9fc849e6825077230","current_canonical":false,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"e8b8e50183c85824927e2f3faa02f7e6ecefa977ae6a5b3e329549119d4a8d58","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/applied-v1/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:11"},{"file_sha256":"eb480d8f8c99523faa2a1d0bc0d2e7fd777579fca63ea66aea829325cb1b4c45","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/applied-v2/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:14"},{"file_sha256":"eb480d8f8c99523faa2a1d0bc0d2e7fd777579fca63ea66aea829325cb1b4c45","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/applied-v2/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:14"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"EO 10634 authorizes Defense Production Act section 302 disaster loans for defense-required facilities, with the determination made by the Director of the Office of Defense Mobilization. The first held end is its express revocation by EO 12148 section 5-106, effective July 15, 1979 by that order's own words (signed July 20, 1979). Successor-office orders EO 10773, EO 11051 and EO 11725 redirect references or functions and are recorded as later events in the reason, not as ends. Searches: the U.S. Code and eCFR searches record two citation hits each for this order without a located text slice in the dossier, so they were not treated as evidence of continuation; the OFR disposition-note search lists the EO 12148 revocation.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Checked for an earlier end: (1) the order has no sunset or condition ending it; (2) EO 10773, EO 11051 and EO 11725 change the responsible office but do not revoke or terminate the order, so they are later events and not ends; (3) no held source shows an earlier lapse of the Defense Production Act section 302 loan authority; (4) EO 12148 names EO 10634 and revokes it, effective July 15, 1979.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The loan authority itself is conferred by section 302 of the Defense Production Act of 1950, which the order invokes; that statute is independent of the order and is not held in the packet. The order adds only the direction that such loans may be made for major-disaster facilities determined by the Director to be required for national defense.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 12148 contains no savings sentence for this revocation in the revocation provision; loans already made under the order would remain governed by their own terms and by the statute, but held evidence shows no continuing right or duty that arises only from the order, so none is recorded.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause covers the whole order. Its only commands are creation of the board, a thirty-day report deadline and a recited statutory standstill. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 (report deadline, option C) none of those ends the order; the first held end is the express revocation by EO 12553 on 1986-02-25 (signature date, no effective-date wording). No board report is held; no spent-fact later event is recorded. Savings: none found in EO 12553 or in the order itself. Searches: U.S. Code and eCFR number-citation searches found no hit for this order; the OFR disposition-note search lists only the EO 12553 revocation in the chain.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Checked for an earlier end: (1) the order's own terms contain a report deadline and a post-report standstill but no sunset, so the report and standstill dates are spent facts and not ends under the report-deadline ruling; (2) no extension, amendment or revocation of EO 10693 other than EO 12553 appears in the dossier chain or in the number-citation scan of held texts; the report-time extensions described in the held report (where a report is held) were stipulations approved by the President, not held instruments, and affect only the deadline; (3) a general later rule is not treated as an end, only EO 12553, which names the order by number.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The standstill is recited as provided by section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, so any no-change duty it describes arises under that statute and not only from the order; by the order's own terms it ran only to thirty days after the board's report, which was long before 1986. The order creates no continuing right, office or appropriation. No other command in the order survives the board's report.\",\"savings_check\":\"The text of EO 12553 contains no savings, exception, effective-date or notwithstanding wording (the held text was searched for those terms). The board's investigation, report and the standstill were spent in the 1950s, and the order created no accrued right or later-effective duty. The statute the order invokes is not affected by revoking the order.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause covers the whole order; the order has no partial-scope command, no exception and no delayed effective date. The revocation in EO 12553 has no exception text, so it reaches the whole order.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"e9c80d0c381d5045f956cd6a0abb24f9683079df4265dbb4c01a66e1e97cdc1a","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:17","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"e9c80d0c381d5045f956cd6a0abb24f9683079df4265dbb4c01a66e1e97cdc1a","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:17","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10693#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10693#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"cbe6b81593b7cfe83dac978bbb2ae7195f16104112d17b2ed5c8246df8d53543","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10693.txt","sha256":"cbe6b81593b7cfe83dac978bbb2ae7195f16104112d17b2ed5c8246df8d53543","state":"match"},"sha256":"3a5794f2b8d5eeb27154965cd4f89679e24dc8a4e288c912f748f6182616946c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10693.txt","text_sha256":"cbe6b81593b7cfe83dac978bbb2ae7195f16104112d17b2ed5c8246df8d53543"}]}]}

chronology does not decide legal effect.","provision_text":"=== PDF page 25 of 256 ===\nFederal Register / Vol. 44, No. 192 / Tuesday, October 2, 1979 / Presidential Documents 56679\n(b) There are allocated to the Secretary of Defense funds made available for \ncarrying out Part II of the A ct (except chapters 4 and 6 thereof).\n(c) There are allocated to the Secretary of State funds made available for \ncarrying out sections 481 and 637(b) and chapter 6 of Part II of the A ct.\n1-802. R ea llo ca tion  o f  Funds. The Director of ID C A , the Secretary of Defense, \nand the Secretary of State may allocate or transfer as appropriate any funds \nreceived under subsections (a), (b), and (c), respectively of section 1-801 of \nthis order, to any agency or part thereof for obligation or expenditure thereby \nconsistent with applicable law.\n1-9. GEN ERAL P R O V ISIO N S\n1-901. Definition. A s used in this order, the word “ function” includes any duty, \nobligation, power, authority, responsibility, right, privilege, discretion, or activ­\nity*\n1-902. R eferen ces to O rd ers and A c ts . {"confidence":"medium","events":[{"actor_id":"EO:11120","char_end":4545,"char_start":4319,"confidence":"medium","how_named":"parent_as_amended","id":"CHAIN:EO:10989#1","notes":"Change wording lead; chronology does not decide legal effect.","provision_text":"Executive Order No. 10168 of October 11,1950, as amended, \nis further amended as follows:\n(a) The title is amended to read as follows:\n“Regulations  R elating  to S pecial  P ay for S ea D uty  and D uty  at\nCertain  P laces”.","sections_touched":["partial"],"signing_date":"1963-10-02","source":{"filing_date":null,"location_confidence":"high","location_verified":false,"page_or_item":"28 FR 10631, PDF pages 3-4","requires_page_check":false,"sha256":"b3ed57a14eb5180999faa2bcf7af4a83e99a7890b99d0b289609cc1cf365d9a9","signing_date":"1963-10-02","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11120.txt","text_sha256":"19e9be86c1ad84be3a0499828e25d18c99d85c6ebd0fdb51e1ee3ead5c51029e","url":"https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-1963-10-03/pdf/FR-1963-10-03.pdf"},"verb":"amend"},{"actor_id":"EO:11157","char_end":32610,"char_start":22638,"confidence":"medium","how_named":"direct","id":"CHAIN:EO:10989#2","notes":"Change wording lead; chronology does not decide legal effect.","provision_text":"Sec. 303. As used in regulations prescribed pursuant to section \n302 hereof, those terms of the said section 402 of title 37 of the \nUnited States Code which are quoted in the subsections of this section \nshall have the meaning or application stated with respect thereto:\n(a) The term “entitled to receive basic pay” shall be considered \napplicable to members while they are on the active list or while they \nare required to perform duty in accordance with law for which they \nare entitled to basic pay: Provided , that such term shall not be \napplicable to any member while absent from duty under conditions \nwhich, under laws governing the particular service concerned, would \nprevent him from receiving full basic pay.\n(b) The term “when rations in kind are not available” shall be \nconsidered applicable in the case of enlisted members on duty at sta­\ntions where it is determined, in accordance with regulations prescribed \npursuant to section 302 hereof, that it is impracticable for subsist­\nence in kind to be furnished by the United States.\n(c) The term “when permission to mess separately is granted” \nshall be considered applicable in the case of enlisted members on duty \nat stations or while sick in hospitals where a mess for subsisting \nenlisted members is available and when such enlisted members are \nauthorized to subsist themselves independently. Such term shall also \nbe considered applicable in the case of enlisted members during all \nperiods of authorized leave, including periods of leave or delay while \nen route between duty stations.\n(d) The term “when assigned to duty under emergency conditions \nwhere no messing facilities of the United States are available” shall \nbe considered applicable in the case of enlisted members assigned to \nduty under conditions requiring extraordinary expenses for subsistence \nas determined in accordance with regulations prescribed pursuant to \nsection 302 hereof.\n(e) The term “being subsisted at the expense of the United States” \nshall be considered applicable to enlisted members who are subsisted \nin kind by the United States and to enlisted members while they are in \na travel status and are entitled to a per-diem allowance in lieu of \nsubsistence or to a mileage allowance.\n\n=== PDF page 13 of 80 ===\nWednesday, June 24, 1964 FEDERAL REGISTER 7979\nP aht I V —Basic  A llowances  for Q uarters\nS ec. 401. As used in this p a rt:\n(a) The term “entitled to receive basic pay” shall apply to a mem­\nber while on the active list or while required to perform duty in accord­\nance with law for which he is entitled to basic pay: Provided , That such \nterm shall not apply to any member while absent from duty under \nconditions which, under laws governing the particular service con­\ncerned, would prevent him from receiving full Dasic pay.\n(b) The term “field duty” shall mean service by a member under \norders with troops operating against an enemy, actual or potential, or \nservice with troops on maneuvers, war games, field exercises, or similar \ntypes of operations.\n(c) The term “sea duty” shall mean service performed by either \nofficer or enlisted members under conditions for which “sea duty” pay \nis payable to enlisted members in accordance with section 305 of title \n37 of the United States Code, and regulations issued thereunder.\n(d) The term “permanent station” shall mean the place on shore \nwhere a member is assigned to duty, or the home yard or the home port \nof a ship in which a member is required to perform duty, under orders \nin each case which do not in terms provide for the termination thereof; \nand any station on shore or any receiving ship where a member is as­\nsigned and in fact occupies, with his dependents, if any, quarters under\n- the jurisdiction of any of the uniformed services shall also be deemed \nduring such occupancy to be his permanent station: Provided , That in \nthe case of members of the National Guard, the Air National Guard \nor reserve components of any of the uniformed services on active duty \nfor training, the place where the training duty is being qierformed \nshall be deemed to be the permanent station of such members for the \npurposes of these regulations.\nS ec. 402. Except as otherwise by statute heretofore or hereafter \nprovided, a member shall be entitled to payment of basic allowances \nfor quarters, in accordance with these regulations and any regulations \nprescribed pursuant hereto, during shell time or times as he is entitled \nto receive basic pay.\nS ec. 403. Any quarters or housing facilities under the jurisdiction \nof any of the uniformed services in fact occupied without payment of \nrental charges (a) by a member and his dependents, or (b) at his \npermanent station by a member without dependents, or (c) by the \ndependents of a member on field duty or on sea duty or on duty at a \nstation where adequate quarters are not available for his dependents, \nshall be deemed to have been assigned to such member as appropriate \nand adequate quarters, and no basic allowance for quarters shall \naccrue to such member under such circumstances unless the occupancy\n(i) occurs while such member is in a duty or leave status incident to \na change of/ permanent station and is of a temporary nature under \nstandards prescribed by regulations issued by the Secretary of Defense \nin the case of members of the Army, Navy, Air Force, or Marine \nCorps, and the reserve components thereof, or by the appropriate \nSecretary in the case of members of the other uniformed services, or\n(ii) occurs while such member is in a leave status not incident to a \nchange of permanent station and does not exceed seven consecutive \ndays at one location: Provided , That occupancy of quarters under such \ncircumstances for a period in excess of such 7-day period or such other \ntemporary period as may be authorized under standards prescribed \nby regulations issued by the Secretary concerned shall not result in \na forfeiture of basic allowance for quarters for such 7-day or other \nauthorized period: Provided further , That this paragraph shall not \napply to occupancy of quarters as a guest of another member.\nS ec. 404. When adequate quarters for his dependents are not avail­\nable for assignment at his perfnanent station to a member with de­\npendents, he may occupy quarters of the United States designated for \nmembers without dependents without affecting his right to receive \npayment of basic allowances for quarters, if permitted or required to \noccupy quarters at such station. Under such circumstances, a member \nmay not occupy quarters of the United States which exceed the mini­\nmum standards for members of his grade without dependents, as\n\n=== PDF page 14 of 80 ===\n7980 THÉ PRESIDENT\nprescribed by the Secretary concerned, unless the only quarters avail­\nable (a) exceed the minimum standards, and (b) are made available \nfor joint occupancy with other members.\nS ec. 405. A member away from his permanent station may occupy \nquarters of the United States designated for members without de­\npendents at his temporary duty station without affecting his right to \nreceive payment of basic allowances for quarters or assignment of \nquarters, if any, at his permanent station. Under such circumstances, \na member may not occupy quarters of the United States which exceed \nthe minimum standards for members of his grade without dependents, \nas prescribed by the Secretary concerned, unless the only quarters \navailable (a) exceed the minimum standards, and (b) are made avail­\nable for joint occupancy with other members.\nS ec. 406. A member serving outside the United States, its territories, \nor possessions in a duty assignment which has official or diplomatic \nresponsibilities involving officials of foreign governments may be \nassigned quarters in excess of the minimum standards set forth in \nsections 404 and 405 hereof, as prescribed by the Secretary concerned: \nProvided , That no such quarters shall be available on a continuous \nbasis for single occupancy, if such quarters are otherwise adequate for \nassignment as family housing to members of similar rank.\nS ec. 407. The Secretaries concerned (within the meaning of sec­\ntion 101(5) of title 37 of the United States Code), with respect to per­\nsonnel of the uniformed services within their respective departments, \nare hereby authorized to prescribe such supplementary regulations not \ninconsistent herewith as they may deem necessary or desirable for \ncarrying out these regulations, and such supplementary regulations \nshall be uniform for all the services to the fullest extent practicable.\nS ec. 408. Unless the Secretary concerned, /or his designee, deter­\nmines that military operational conditions require otherwise, a com­\nmissioned officer without dependents who is in a pay grade above pay \ngrade 0 -3  and who is assigned to quarters of the United States or a \nhousing facility under the jurisdiction of a uniformed service, appro­\npriate to his grade or rank and adequate for himself, may elect not to \noccupy those quarters and instead to receive the basic allowance for \nquarters prescribed for his pay grade by section 403 of title 37 of the. \nUnited States Code.\nP art Y — General  P rovisions\nS ec. 501. For the purposes of these regulations, the terms defined \nin sections 101 and 401 of title 37 of the United States Code shall have \nthe meanings prescribed therein.\nS ec. 502. The following Executive orders are revoked:\n(a) Executive Order No. 10119 of March 27,1950.\n(b) Executive Order No. 10152 of August 17,1950.\n(c) Executive Order No. 10168 of October 11,1950.\n(d) Executive Order No. 10204 of January 15,1951.\n(e) Executive Order No. 10605 of April 22,1955.\n( f ) Executive Order No. 10618 of June 28,1955.\n(g) Executive Order No. 10681 of October 22,1956.\n(h) Executive Order No. 10739 of November 15,1957.\n(i) Executive Order No. 10821 of May 20,1959.\n(j ) Executive Order No. 10892 of November 8,1960.\n(k) Executive Order No. 10989 of January 22,1962.\n","sections_touched":["section \n302","section 402","section 302","section 302","section 305","sections 404","section 403","sections 101"],"signing_date":"1964-06-22","source":{"filing_date":null,"location_confidence":"high","location_verified":false,"page_or_item":"29 FR 7973, PDF pages 7-14","requires_page_check":false,"sha256":"c9f34554598abe9c9ae9d577110d372940bd0af4c0d820a755225f96316aa79f","signing_date":"1964-06-22","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11157.txt","text_sha256":"945bb010cca24982014e2cbbdedae417345ba1266732bd772aa5895e9a294634","url":"https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-1964-06-24/pdf/FR-1964-06-24.pdf"},"verb":"revoke"}],"generic_refs_excluded":221,"id":"CHAIN:EO:10989","notes":"Named, located change provisions only; missing provisions and unheld texts remain dossier gaps. Kennedy) dated 1962-01-22; the number, subject and date agree with the census register row (subject: Amendment of Executive Order No. 10168, of October 11, 1950, as amended, prescribing regulations relating to the right of enlisted members of the uniformed services to additional pay for sea and forei). I read the whole text and signature block. Inventory: the dossier's three clauses (the lead-in, the amendment adding clauses (5) and (6) to section 2(a), and the amendment of section 3 with the effective-date sentence) are contiguous and cover every operative sentence; I confirm the inventory as complete. The order further amends Executive Order No. 10168 of October 11, 1950, as amended (special pay for sea duty and duty at certain places for enlisted members): it adds two new clauses to section 2(a) treating duty on an artificial island such as a Texas Tower on the outer Continental Shelf as sea duty (clause 5 for assignment there, clause 6 for duty there while based ashore for eight consecutive days or more), amends section 3 to refer to clauses (3), (5) and (6), and states that the amendments are effective as of the first day of the first month after the month of issue, that is 1962-02-01. Ending: Executive Order 11157 of June 22, 1964 (29 FR 7973, Federal Register of June 24, 1964), section 502, states \"The following Executive orders are revoked:\" and its item (k) reads \"(k) Executive Order No. 10989 of January 22,1962.\" Item (c) of the same section also revokes Executive Order No. 10168 itself. The held Federal Register text has no effective-date wording; the signature line's year is garbled in the OCR (it prints 1961 with a stray mark), but the same text prints \"Filed, June 22,1964\", the Federal Register issue is dated June 24, 1964, and the American Presidency Project record gives June 22, 1964, so I read the order as signed on 1964-06-22 and effective on signing. The alternative reading is the publication date, 1964-06-24. The cited source for the end is the complete held Federal Register text of EO 11157 (the dossier's located-provision records for this pair use a transcription mirror, which I did not rely on); section 502 is on its own lines in that text. Savings: EO 11157 restates the artificial-island clauses in its own section 202(a)(5) and (6) (it reads \"While assigned to an artificial island (such as a Texas Tower)\") and in section 203, and has no express savings clause (searched); pay accrued before the revocation is an accrued consequence. Searches selected after reading their coverage notes (all three complete, no coverage warning): U.S. Code release point 119-121 (no hit for this order), eCFR as of 2026-10-14 (no hit), and the Federal Register catalog disposition notes (2 machine hits, the revocation note on this order and the matching note on EO 12553).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The own text sets no end. An intervening amendment of the parent, Executive Order 11120 of 1963-10-02, amends the title, section 2(a)(2) and sections 4 to 7 of Executive Order 10168 and does not change section 2(a)(5), section 2(a)(6) or section 3; it is a later event on the parent, not an end of these clauses, and the strike-and-insert ruling of 2026-10-21 treats such amendments as later events. No held source shows any other earlier revocation, supersession or expiry. The first ending shown by held evidence is the express revocation by number in section 502(k) of EO 11157, effective 1964-06-22.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each clause only amends Executive Order 10168; none is an independent command. The parent order 10168 was itself revoked by item (c) of the same section 502 on the same date, so no surviving parent text carries these clauses.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 11157 has no express savings clause (a search of the text for saving, prior to, nothing in and heretofore found none; the one match for heretofore is in section 402 on another subject). Its sections 202(a)(5)-(6) and 203 restate the same sea-duty rule for artificial islands, so the substance continued under the new order and entitlements accrued before 1964-06-22 are accrued consequences; none is claimed as a continuing command of this order.\",\"scope_check\":\"Three clauses cover the whole order and all three end on the same date because EO 11157 section 502(k) revokes the order itself, not a section of it. The effective-date sentence sits within the third clause and ends with it.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"5de0260d810af410db7ee710f4d0ceeb20eee3c8ac2ec307b317f9a0a261c311","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:2","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"5de0260d810af410db7ee710f4d0ceeb20eee3c8ac2ec307b317f9a0a261c311","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:2","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10989#lead"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10989#lead"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10989#1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10989#1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10989#2"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10989#2"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"945bb010cca24982014e2cbbdedae417345ba1266732bd772aa5895e9a294634","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11157.txt","sha256":"945bb010cca24982014e2cbbdedae417345ba1266732bd772aa5895e9a294634","state":"match"},"sha256":"c9f34554598abe9c9ae9d577110d372940bd0af4c0d820a755225f96316aa79f","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11157.txt","text_sha256":"945bb010cca24982014e2cbbdedae417345ba1266732bd772aa5895e9a294634"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"dffc239614b4f3d1d5362ce48f6f3fdf6f3cbb8aaa283b74ebaa7fe5761f6adb","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10989.txt","sha256":"dffc239614b4f3d1d5362ce48f6f3fdf6f3cbb8aaa283b74ebaa7fe Kennedy) dated 1962-04-07; the number, subject and date agree with the census register row (subject: Creating a board of inquiry to report on a labor dispute affecting the maritime industry of the United States). I read the whole clause and the signature block. Inventory: the dossier's single whole-order clause covers the recitals and every operative sentence; nothing outside it is a command (any attached lists of carriers or unions identify parties and are not commands). The one operative clause creates a Board of Inquiry under section 206 of the Labor Management Relations Act, 1947 (29 U.S.C. 176) to inquire into the labor dispute described in the recitals, names its members, gives it the powers and duties of Title II of that Act, directs it to report to the President on or before the stated date, and provides that after its report the Board continues in existence to perform other functions required under the Act until the Board is terminated by the President. Ending: EO 12553 of February 25, 1986, titled \"Revocation of Various Executive Orders\", orders that the listed Executive Orders, and \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" and its table lists this order by number and subject on one line: \"11013 Creating a board of inquiry to report on a labor dispute affecting the maritime industry\". EO 12553 contains no effective-date wording (the held text was searched for effective, take effect, shall become, except, notwithstanding, nothing in, provided that and saving, with no hit outside table subjects); it is headed as an order of February 25, 1986 and signed that day, so the date is read as 1986-02-25, the signing date. The alternative reading is the Federal Register publication date, 1986-03-03 (Monday, March 3, 1986, 51 FR 7237); no later date is supported by the text. The quoted source for the end is the enacting paragraph of EO 12553, which names no order; the order is named by its row in the No./Subject table, quoted above, so the table row and the enacting paragraph are read together. The report date is a deadline, not an end. The order also makes the Board's continued existence last until the Board is terminated by the President. No held source records any presidential act terminating the Board before EO 12553, so that contingent own-terms ending is not evidenced; under the owner ruling of 2026-10-28 an unestablished earlier event is not claimed, and the express revocation is the first ending shown by held evidence. The unevidenced possibility of an earlier termination is disclosed as a point for the human reviewer. Earlier candidates: a search of the held order texts for citations of this order by number found no later order that revokes, amends or extends it; the Office of the Federal Register catalog notes name EO 12553 as the revoking order. Savings: EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception, and the order created no continuing right or obligation outside its own terms. Searches selected after reading their coverage notes (all three complete, no coverage warning): U.S. Code release point 119-121 (no hit), eCFR as of 2026-10-14 (no hit), and the Federal Register catalog disposition notes (2 machine hits, the revocation note on this order and the matching note on EO 12553).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms periods (report deadline and standstill) are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21. No held source shows an earlier revocation, supersession, expiry or termination. The first ending shown by held evidence is the express revocation by number in EO 12553, effective 1986-02-25. The Board's contingent termination by the President is not evidenced before EO 12553. Later orders that merely recite or list this order would not be ends; none was found that does more than EO 12553.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order has no command independent of the board: the standstill is recited from the statute and depends on the board's creation and report. No other command in the order, and no other order that incorporates it, was found in the held texts.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception (searched as described); the board's report and any settlement or later legislation are not obligations created by this order and are not claimed as surviving effects.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, whole order. The dossier's inventory spans the recitals, the creation of the board, the report command and the statutory standstill recital to the signature; I confirm it as complete. EO 12553 revokes the whole order (it lists the order itself and its amendments), so no part of the order is left outside the ending.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"5de0260d810af410db7ee710f4d0ceeb20eee3c8ac2ec307b317f9a0a261c311","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:6","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"5de0260d810af410db7ee710f4d0ceeb20eee3c8ac2ec307b317f9a0a261c311","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:6","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:11013#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:11013#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"48b1c55398b6ff8851b88974b3510455c643c5694af704507e0d64a682cf83a1","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11013.txt","sha256":"48b1c55398b6ff8851b88974b3510455c643c5694af704507e0d64a682cf83a1","state":"match"},"sha256":"a3810eb91001cc6c963c8cacca5729931fd6786bec359f0bcfcbf29a1639bb2b","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11013.txt","text_sha256":"48b1c55398b6ff8851b88974b3510455c643c5694af704507e0d64a682cf83a1"}]}]}

Kennedy) dated 1962-06-08; the number, subject and date agree with the census register row (subject: Amendment of Executive Order No. 11025, creating a Board of Inquiry to report on a labor dispute affecting the aircraft industry of the United States). I read the whole clause and signature block. Inventory: the dossier's single whole-order clause covers every operative sentence; I confirm it as complete. The one operative clause amends the first paragraph of Executive Order No. 11025 of June 7, 1962 (the Board of Inquiry on the aircraft-industry dispute) to read as a new recital naming the parties to the dispute at the Republic Aviation Corporation facility in Farmingdale, New York, and a cafeteria concessionaire there. It contains no command of its own beyond that rewriting and no date or condition of its own. Ending: EO 12553 of February 25, 1986, titled \"Revocation of Various Executive Orders\", orders that the listed Executive Orders, and \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" Its table lists this order by number and subject on one line: \"11026 Amending Executive Order No. 11025 creating a board of inquiry to report on a labor dispute affecting\". EO 12553 contains no effective-date wording (the held text was searched for effective, take effect, shall become, except, notwithstanding, nothing in, provided that and saving, with no hit outside table subjects); it is headed as an order of February 25, 1986 and signed that day, so the date is read as 1986-02-25, the signing date. The alternative reading is the Federal Register publication date, 1986-03-03 (Monday, March 3, 1986, 51 FR 7237); no later date is supported by the text. The quoted source for the end is the enacting paragraph of EO 12553, which names no order; the order is named by its row in the No./Subject table, quoted above, so the table row and the enacting paragraph are read together. Because the order only rewrites the text of EO 11025, it has no performance date, report deadline or sunset of its own; the amendment was effective when made and the rewritten text then formed part of EO 11025. EO 11025 is also listed in EO 12553 (its row reads \"11025 Creating a board of inquiry to report on a labor dispute affecting the aircraft industry\"). A later order, EO 11029 of 1962-06-13, again amended EO 11025, changing its title and substituting new text for its first and second paragraphs. I read the complete held Federal Register copy of EO 11029 (27 FR 5699): it names Executive Order No. 11025 and does not name EO 11026. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on unnamed instruments, an order that does not name the target does not end it, and under the strike-and-insert ruling of the same date an amendment is a later event rather than an end. The 1962-06-13 rewording of the same first paragraph is therefore disclosed here as an earlier candidate, not claimed as the end; its source is the held text of EO 11029, which is not one of the cited sources of this claim. Savings: EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception. Searches selected after reading their coverage notes (all three complete, no coverage warning): U.S. Code release point 119-121 (no hit for this order), eCFR as of 2026-10-14 (no hit), and the Federal Register catalog disposition notes (2 machine hits, the revocation note on this order and the matching note on EO 12553).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No held source shows an earlier revocation, supersession, expiry or termination of this order. The own text has no end. A later order, EO 11029 of 1962-06-13, again amended EO 11025, changing its title and substituting new text for its first and second paragraphs. I read the complete held Federal Register copy of EO 11029 (27 FR 5699): it names Executive Order No. 11025 and does not name EO 11026. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on unnamed instruments, an order that does not name the target does not end it, and under the strike-and-insert ruling of the same date an amendment is a later event rather than an end. The 1962-06-13 rewording of the same first paragraph is therefore disclosed here as an earlier candidate, not claimed as the end; its source is the held text of EO 11029, which is not one of the cited sources of this claim. The first ending shown by held evidence is the express revocation by number in EO 12553, effective 1986-02-25. Whether the Board of Inquiry created by EO 11025 ended earlier is a question for the claim on EO 11025 and does not supply an end for this separate amending order.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order has no command independent of the text it amends; it creates no board, duty, deadline or authority of its own. No other order incorporating it was found in the held texts.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception (searched as described). Kennedy) dated 1962-07-11; the number, subject and date agree with the census register row (subject: Administration of the Agricultural Trade Development and Assistance Act of 1954, as amended). I read the whole text and signature block. Inventory: the dossier splits the order into two contiguous clauses at an OCR break inside amended Section 4(a)(1) (the second clause begins with a sentence fragment labelled Section 104). Together they cover amendments (I), (II) and (III) and the signature; I confirm the inventory as complete and decide both clauses as parts of one amending order. The order further amends Executive Order No. 10900 of January 5, 1961, as amended (administration of the Agricultural Trade Development and Assistance Act of 1954): (I) it substitutes a new Section 4(a), under which the Director of the Bureau of the Budget fixes the amounts of foreign currencies accruing under Title I to be used for section 104 purposes and notifies the Secretary of the Treasury, and the waiver function of the penultimate proviso of section 104 is delegated to the Secretary of State for section 104(e) and to the Director of the Bureau of the Budget otherwise; (II) it inserts a new paragraph (5) in Section 4(d) assigning section 104(s) purposes to the Department of the Treasury in consultation with the Department of State and delegating the terms-and-conditions function to the Secretary of the Treasury; and (III) it substitutes a new Section 4(d)(7) assigning section 104(g) purposes to the Department of State and delegating the loan-manner function to the Secretary of State. Ending: Executive Order 12220 of June 27, 1980 (45 FR 44245, Federal Register of July 1, 1980; the complete held Federal Register text) provides in section 1-501 that Executive Order No. 10900 of January 5, 1961, as amended, is revoked. Because this order's text is part of Executive Order 10900 as amended, that revocation reaches it, and the same order re-delegates each function in revised form: section 1-401(a) addresses the use of foreign currencies, the fixing of amounts by the Director of the Office of Management and Budget and the notice to the Treasury, section 1-401(b) re-delegates the waiver function (to the Director of the United States International Development Cooperation Agency for section 104(e) and to the Director of the Office of Management and Budget otherwise), section 1-405(a) re-delegates the loan-manner function formerly in Section 4(d)(7), and section 1-405(b) re-delegates the terms-and-conditions function formerly in Section 4(d)(5) to the Secretary of the Treasury in consultation with the Secretary of State. The held text has no effective-date wording and is headed as an order of June 27, 1980, so I read 1980-06-27 as the effective date; the alternative reading is the publication date, 1980-07-01. The dossier's located-provision record for this pair uses a transcription mirror of EO 12220; I did not rely on it and cite the Federal Register text instead. Savings: the functions continue under Executive Order 12220, which re-delegates them in revised form; EO 12220 has no express savings clause in the held text (reviewed in full). Searches selected after reading their coverage notes (all three complete, no coverage warning): U.S. Code release point 119-121 (no hit for this order), eCFR as of 2026-10-14 (no hit), and the Federal Register catalog disposition notes (2 machine hits, the revocation note on this order and the matching note on EO 12553).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The own text sets no end. I checked each intervening instrument in the dossier. Executive Order 11252 (1965-10-20) transfers the functions of the Director of the Food-For-Peace Program to the Secretary of State and modifies Executive Order 10900 accordingly; the functions delegated here run to the Budget Director, the Treasury and State, not that Director. Executive Order 11963 (1977-01-19) adds a subsection to Section 1 of Executive Order 10900. Executive Order 12148 (1979-07-20), in section 5-108, revokes only Section 4(d)(2) of Executive Order 10900, a paragraph this order does not touch. Executive Order 12163 (1979-09-29) amends Section 3 of Executive Order 10900 and revokes other orders. None of these ends these clauses; amendments are later events, not ends. The held text of Executive Order 11836, which the dossier lists as citing this order by number, prints the number 11036 over a different subject (the Transportation Cargo Security Program) and refers to neither Executive Order 10900 nor the Agricultural Trade Development and Assistance Act, so it is not an event for this order. The first ending shown by held evidence is section 1-501 of Executive Order 12220, effective 1980-06-27.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each clause only amends Executive Order 10900; none is a command independent of that order. The parent order was revoked as a whole by section 1-501 of Executive Order 12220, so no surviving parent text carries these amendments.\",\"savings_check\":\"The substance of each delegation continued, in revised form, under sections 1-401 and 1-405 of Executive Order 12220, which also revoked the parent; no continuing command of this order outside that restatement was found, and no express savings clause appears in the held text of Executive Order 12220.\",\"scope_check\":\"Two clauses cover the whole order; amendments (I), (II) and (III) are all part of Executive Order 10900 as Johnson) dated 1964-01-07; the number, subject and date agree with the census register row (subject: Modifying Executive Order No., as amended by Executive Order No. 10103, providing for the transfer of personnel to certain public international organizations). I read the whole clause and signature block. Inventory: the dossier's single whole-order clause covers every operative sentence; I confirm it as complete. The one operative clause modifies Executive Order No. 9721 of May 10, 1946, as amended by Executive Order No. 10103 of February 1, 1950 (transfer of personnel to public international organizations): it waives, with respect to one named person, Frederick Strauss, the Section 1 limit on the period during which a transferred employee is treated as on leave of absence and the Section 3(a) limit on the time within which reemployment rights may be exercised, and provides that he is considered to have been on leave of absence from August 12, 1957 through September 16, 1962 for all rights and benefits under Executive Order 9721, as amended. Ending: EO 12553 of February 25, 1986, titled \"Revocation of Various Executive Orders\", orders that the listed Executive Orders, and \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" Its table lists this order by number and subject on one line: \"11138 Modifying provisions for the transfer of personnel to certain public international organizations\". EO 12553 contains no effective-date wording (the held text was searched for effective, take effect, shall become, except, notwithstanding, nothing in, provided that and saving, with no hit outside table subjects); it is headed as an order of February 25, 1986 and signed that day, so the date is read as 1986-02-25, the signing date. The alternative reading is the Federal Register publication date, 1986-03-03 (Monday, March 3, 1986, 51 FR 7237); no later date is supported by the text. The quoted source for the end is the enacting paragraph of EO 12553, which names no order; the order is named by its row in the No./Subject table, quoted above, so the table row and the enacting paragraph are read together. This is a one-person waiver. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-28, a waiver exhausted by performance is recorded as such only where evidence shows it was used; no held source shows whether or when Mr. Strauss exercised reemployment rights or otherwise used the waiver, so no exhaustion date or interval is claimed and the question is disclosed as open. The waiver's surviving consequence (his deemed leave status and any rights that accrued) is distinct from the permission itself. The express revocation is the first ending shown by held evidence. Savings: EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception; rights that accrued to the named person before the revocation are treated as accrued, not as a continuing independent command. Searches selected after reading their coverage notes (all three complete, no coverage warning): U.S. Code release point 119-121 (no hit for this order), eCFR as of 2026-10-14 (no hit), and the Federal Register catalog disposition notes (2 machine hits, the revocation note on this order and the matching note on EO 12553).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No held source shows an earlier revocation, supersession or expiry; the own text has no end date or condition, and the waiver covers a past period and a time limit rather than a future event. Whether the waiver was earlier exhausted by use is not established by held evidence (owner ruling 2026-10-28: unknown-whether is not claimed as an interval). The first ending shown by held evidence is the express revocation by number in EO 12553, effective 1986-02-25. The parent order EO 9721 is not claimed here and its own status does not end this separate order.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order's only command is the waiver and deeming rule for the named person; there is no independent continuing command. The parent order EO 9721 continues under its own terms and is not part of this claim.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception. Assistant Director (Program Planning, Analysis and Research), Office of Economic Opportunity; General Counsel, Veterans Administration), under section 303(g) of the Government Employees Salary Reform Act of 1964, and takes effect immediately. I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11195.txt, 30 FR 1169, PDF pages 5-5) and confirmed its number, title and date against the census register. The dossier's seven clauses (two amendment leads, four added positions and the effective-date sentence) cover every sentence of the order, so I confirm the inventory. EO 12060 (signed 1978-05-15) revokes the order by number in its section 3, and the dossier carries the ACT record for it. The first end of every clause is that express revocation. Amendments continue until the amended order ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on strike-and-insert amendments, and EO 11189 was revoked by the same sentence. Earlier candidate end that does not name this order, disclosed under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on unnamed instruments (option C; it is not the end because it does not name EO 11195): Pub. L. 89-554 (Sept. 6, 1966, 80 Stat. 378), which enacted title 5 of the United States Code, lists in 5 U.S.C. 5315 the Chief of Protocol, Department of State, as item (73), and in 5 U.S.C. 5316 the Assistant Director (Program Planning, Analysis and Research), Office of Economic Opportunity as item (101), the General Counsel of the Veterans' Administration as item (110) and the Staff Director, Commission on Civil Rights as item (114), so every position this order added appears in the statute (held at jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_11195_Pub._L._89-554_Sept._6_1966_80_Stat._378_et_seq._including_5_U.S.C._5315_and_5316_as__c629b8a1e7.txt). The Federal Register catalog note on EO 11189 says that statute \"Nullified by 80 Stat. 460, 461 (5 U.S.C. 5315, 5316)\" EO 11189 and EO 11195 because it included all positions they added (jobs/later-events/output/CATALOG_INDEX.csv), but the catalog note on EO 11195 itself records only the EO 12060 revocation. Section 7(c) of the same Act says an order in effect under a replaced law continues in effect under the corresponding provision \"until repealed, amended, or superseded\", which points the other way. I did not establish that the statute extinguished this order, so EO 12060 is recorded as the first end. The 5 U.S.C. 5317 note in the U.S. Code release point also records that this order was revoked by EO 12060.
